## 2026-11-11T01:45Z — Engine: wwalk target walk widened to a u128 coefficient word (sib, lazy, cv=0 above r = 2^31) (Claude, main session)


- Owner: "go ahead with the engine change for larger curves". Root causes: `TargetWalk` packed both seed coefficients in one u64 (`u1 << 32 | u2`) with the cv=0/lazy key sign in bit 63; `wwalk_plan_check` refused bounds >= 2^32 for every wwalk; sib refused r >= 2^31 because reduced minus-sibling coefficients (< r) share that word; lazy/cv=0 with tw=2 refused bounds >= 2^31 for the same sign bit.
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_boundary.rs`: the target walk's `u` word is a `u128` (`u1 << 64 | u2`), `TW_SIGN_BIT = 1 << 127`, new `TW_COEF_BITS = 62` and `wwalk_plan_check_bits` (the plain `WalkTable` keeps 32 bits via `wwalk_plan_check`); the three 2^31 refusals removed (sib now refuses r >= 2^62). `ecbench/methods.rs`: the planner uses 62 bits for tw >= 1 and drops its cv 2^31 refusal. `plugins.rs`: cv help text; new test `wwalk_target_walk_runs_above_two_to_the_31` (sib p256/p64, lazy, cv=0 on a = -3 prime-order curves at r ~ 2^34 and 2^38, plus the WalkTable 32-bit refusal).

## Trace-zero k = 2, d = 1 residual: b = 0 curves, and the 2-descent mechanism (Claude)

### What was done
- Checked the scratchpad A6 rows against the pinned SHA-256 (`fa1111c1…`), then stratified them by curve class with `research/tracezero_toy_20261106/stratify_curve_class.py`. k = 2, b = 0 (n = 75): Δcov_3 = −0.2032 [−0.2250, −0.1832]; generic (n = 2,250): +0.0027; the weighted mix equals the pooled −0.0037. The b = 0 deficit is the same for p mod 4 = 1 and 3.
- `subgroup_index_b0.py` (p = 23, 37, 53): for b = 0 curves the subgroup generated by the trace image has index 2 in exactly the (curve, line) cases where N(g) is a square in F_p, and index 1 otherwise; generic curves are index 1 in all 4,400 cases.
- `descent_coset_test.py`: on ordinary curves with a rational 2-torsion point (r, 0) and lines V = r + F_p·g, the 2-isogeny descent class delta(P) = x(P) − r mod squares is constant on the trace image and equals the square class of N(g); index 2 exactly when N(g) is a square. 400/480, 720/720 and 1040/1040 split by class at p = 23, 37, 53; no mixed case.

### Decisions made
- Mechanism: delta is a homomorphism E(F_p) → F_p*/squares, and delta(Tr Q) = N(x(Q) − r) = t²·N(g) on such a line, a constant. Derived after the b = 0 finding; the tests above confirm it on toy fields only.
- Linked to the cofactor-curve question as analysis, not measured on cofactor curves (see README).

### Open threads
- The repo's cofactor-lift oracle `pkm-semaev:lifts=1` (research/curve25519_cofactor_algebraic_20261102) tries all 8 shifts R + K. The delta test would rule out half of them when the base lies on a fixed-class line, but only if the base points' x-classes are fixed. This needs checking on the k = 20 miniature before any claim.
- k = 3, a = 0 Δcov_2 = −0.0133 is one exploratory look; not pursued.

## E-222 / M-221 regimen: why H1 recovers less than 1/h (Claude, fork)

- User said "all": native-size timing and the shortfall investigation. The shortfall analysis ran as a Python-only fork (no git, no cargo) in `research/ic_e222_m221_20261106/h1_shortfall/`.
- Model: a trial succeeds only when R lands on a distinct table point lying in <G>, so E[on/off trials] = (table points in <G> without H1) / (all table points with H1), computed from the actual 32-entry factor bases. It reproduces the earlier medians (E 0.2774, M 0.1525) from regenerated instances.
- Edwards: exact model 0.272 against observed 0.2774, residual +1% (CI includes 0), so fully accounted for. Montgomery: model 0.136 against 0.1525, residual +7% (CI +2.4% to +11.8%), about 63% of the log gap removed; the rest is unexplained and may be noise (96th percentile of the model's spread).
- Mechanisms found: the diagonal points +-2F_i are usable with probability |T[2]|/h instead of 1/h (+0.008 E, +0.004 M); the table collides at small rungs (1,635 distinct points against 1,927 at E rung 12), raising the ratio by about 0.03 there, gone by rung 20. Torsion classes of small-x points are uniform; scan order has no effect.

- Reported the Montgomery residual as unexplained instead of claiming the model closes it.

## 2026-11-11T02:10Z — `ic corpus`: reject degenerate planted sums (Claude, side session of the ic-dataset work)


- Branch `fix/ic-corpus-nondegenerate-plants-20261110` off `origin/main` f13a3b5265, in a sparse worktree (`/Volumes/SSD990-2/crypto-worktrees/ic-corpus-nondegenerate-20261110`); disk had about 6 GB free.
- Confirmed the mechanism in `src/cryptanalysis/ic_corpus.rs::generate`: three points drawn independently, with replacement, from a base holding both `±P` above every abscissa in `V = <1, z, ..., z^(l-1)>`. So `P_j = −P_i` occurs and `R = P_k` is a factor-base point. Repeated points `P + P + Q` also occur.
- Fix: `plant_nondegenerate` redraws until the three abscissae are pairwise distinct (no `P_i = ±P_j`) and `x_R ≥ 2^l`. The cap is 100,000 redraws, and it errors if `V` carries fewer than three abscissae. The `--sat` count is still met. The S4 check and the solver confirmation in `build_entry` are unchanged.
- Every INFO file (U instances included) now has the line `planting v2 three pairwise-distinct factor-base abscissae (no P_i = +-P_j), x_R outside the subspace; degenerate draws redrawn`. Corpora without that line predate the fix. The `ic corpus` JSON report gains `planting`, `planting_redraws_total` and per-instance `planting_redraws`.
- Tests: `plants_are_never_degenerate` (2,000 plants for each of `a = 0, 1` at n = 23, l = 4, asserting no repeated abscissa, no cancelling pair, the recomputed sum equals the target, and `x_R ≥ 2^l`), and `generated_corpus_has_no_degenerate_plants` (`generate` end to end, 40 S + 2 U per curve).
- Before/after at n = 23, l = 4, 150 planted per (a, seed), seeds 1 and 2, release `ic` built from origin/main vs this branch (corpora in the session scratchpad, not in git):

  | a | seed | old x_R < 2^l | old repeated abscissa | new (both) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | 1 | 21/150 (14.0%) | 52/150 (34.7%) | 0/150 |
  | 0 | 2 | 19/150 (12.7%) | 43/150 (28.7%) | 0/150 |
  | 1 | 1 | 42/150 (28.0%) | 81/150 (54.0%) | 0/150 |
  | 1 | 2 | 37/150 (24.7%) | 77/150 (51.3%) | 0/150 |

  Pooled: 119/600 (19.8%) of the old plants had x_R in V; 253/600 (42.2%) had a repeated abscissa. The new generator gave 0/600 on both counts.
- The reported example `ic corpus --degree 47 --dimension 4 --curve-a 0 --seed 7 --sat 2 --unsat 2` now plants witnesses (0xc, 0x8, 0x1) → x_R 0x55010527854d and (0x4, 0xa, 0xe) → x_R 0x381df7b8be8. Both were degenerate before.
- Sources checked: KB `source:62c9bb344e4a32fe66e1d14e278246d5` (crypto-autoresearcher `knowledge/literature/KN-LIT-036049.md`, the Trimoska–Ionica–Dequen EC-Index-Calculus-Benchmarks generator: "samples P_1, P_2, P_3 ∈ V_l, sets R = P_1 + P_2 + P_3", with no non-degeneracy condition stated) and `source:9c05118a98ff15631b640df2ecacc3d3` (`research/notes/ecc2k130/RESEARCH_TRIMOSKA_BENCHMARKS.md`, "Planted-solution benchmarks"). The weight-skew finding that motivated the fix is ml-cryptanalysis `experiments/ic-models-20261105/README.md` (commit ea96326e).


- Repeated equal points (`P + P + Q`) are rejected too. `2P + Q` is a valid relation, but the INFO witness records abscissae only, so `P + P` and `P − P` are indistinguishable there. A downstream filter could not verify a plant, and a repeated root is itself a visible witness feature. At l = 4 this removes about 30-50% of the old draws, so v2 planted targets are sums of three distinct factor-base abscissae only.
- Degenerate draws are redrawn rather than filtered, so `--sat N` still yields N. This consumes a different amount of the random stream, so a given seed now yields different S *and* U instances than before v2.
- The `label_certified_by` string is unchanged (downstream may match it). Versioning is the new `planting` line. ml-cryptanalysis `csd/lab/ic_dataset/systems.py::parse_info` ignores unknown keys.


- Existing corpora (ml-cryptanalysis ic-solves v1/v2/v3, PDP-SAT synthesis sets) still contain degenerate plants. They are filtered on x_R < 2^l downstream. Filtering on x_R alone does not remove the old P+P+Q plants. At n = 23, l = 4: 134/600 (22.3%) old plants had a repeated abscissa and x_R >= 2^l. All 119 x_R < 2^l plants also had a repeated abscissa; none had three distinct abscissae.
- The committed `docs/ic/corpus/{n15l5,n19l6}` S instances on main are non-degenerate (checked: distinct X, x_R >= 2^l); no CI step regenerates them.
- No PR opened (not asked).


- Homebrew `cargo`/`rustc` 1.93.1 come first on PATH and are too old for the lockfile (aws-* need 1.94.1), and 1.94.1 lacks a feature the lib uses. Built with the rustup `stable` toolchain (1.99.1) by putting `~/.rustup/toolchains/stable-aarch64-apple-darwin/bin` first on PATH.
- The sparse worktree initially missed files pulled in by `include_str!`. Added them as non-cone patterns after scanning every include macro.

## 2026-11-11 — Per-replay revision in the frozen-workflow index; P-192 Lean workflow stays `record` (Claude)


- `git fetch --unshallow --filter=blob:none origin main`, then hashed every blob of `.github/workflows/p192-geometry-collector-lean.yml` in `git log --all`. `07024051…` is held by `3283d46e4a`, `c508792ade`, `b94618d7a0` (and the index revision `d30a63b89c`); `b350582e…` is held only by `d0597e0ea3` ("Construct eightfold P-192 cross-cubic anchor relations", an ancestor of main).
- Of the three `ARTIFACTS.sha256` manifests the workflow checks, only `reports/run-20261024-anchor-eight/` pins the workflow. `run-20261024` and `run-20261024-high-degree` do not, and `sha256sum -c` passes on both on live bytes.

## 2026-11-11T05:50Z — bench-records `records` job: --verify-s3 ran out the 15-minute timeout (Claude)


- Diagnosed why `records` in `.github/workflows/bench-records.yml` is cancelled at `timeout-minutes: 15`. Job 130270447782 (PR #3871) logged its last line at 02:09:54, in `benchrec.py check --base origin/main --verify-s3`, and was cancelled at 02:19:44. Cleanup killed orphan `python3` and `aws` processes.
- Cause, measured from this container against the real bucket: `--verify-s3` started one `aws s3api head-object` process per raw object, serially. 20 HEADs took 0.68 s each with region us-east-2 (the bucket's region; `get-bucket-location` says `us-east-2`) and 1.05 s each with us-west-2, the `AWS_REGION` CI exports (`aws_env()` used `setdefault`, so CI's us-west-2 won). Records on main named 312 raw objects on 11-05, 584 on 11-08, 1,137 on 11-10 and 1,504 at 01:50Z on 11-11 (1,561 now). At 0.7–1.0 s each, the loop needs about 18–27 minutes. Push runs on main were already cancelled on 11-10, when the count passed about 900–1,300.
- Fix (`tools/benchrec.py`): `S3Inventory` lists the store prefix once (`list-objects-v2`, keys and sizes; 21,148 objects in 3.6–3.8 s) when ≥16 wanted keys sit under one prefix. Otherwise, or if the listing is refused, it runs HEADs, 16 at a time. Every `aws` call goes through `aws_call()`, which adds `--cli-connect-timeout 10`, `--cli-read-timeout 60`, `--region us-east-2`, stdin from /dev/null, `AWS_PAGER=""` and a hard subprocess limit. On timeout it raises `S3Timeout`, which `main()` prints as `FAIL: S3 could not be verified: <command> did not finish within Ns`. `--verify-s3` also compares the stored size with the recorded `bytes` (stricter than before).
- Tests: 8 new in `tools/test_benchrec.py` (one listing replaces per-object HEADs; region and timeouts on every call; missing object; wrong size; refused listing falls back to HEADs with the same coverage; few objects use HEADs; hung listing and hung HEAD fail in seconds with a clear error). `python3 -m unittest tools/test_benchrec.py`: 37 tests OK.
- New check locally against real S3 with `AWS_REGION=us-west-2`: 7.3 s wall, S3 inventory 3.8 s, `1 prefix listing(s) (21148 objects), 0 HEAD(s)`. Docs: `docs/benchmark-records.md`.


- Listing replaces HEADs only for presence and size; a refused listing (no `s3:ListBucket`) falls back to HEADs so coverage never drops. A *timed-out* listing fails the check instead of falling back, so the total time stays bounded.
- Timeouts raise instead of returning "absent", so a slow S3 is never misreported as missing data or a differing raw file.


- With the check now finishing, it reports 17 real problems already on main, merged while the job was being cancelled. Each needs its owner, and they will keep `records` red on every PR until fixed:
  - 3 records still `open` on main: `20261109T1453Z-trace-line-reference-isolab` (branch claude/happy-johnson-9edk2r), `20261109T2031Z-pkm-fd-pfd-b`, `20261110T0305Z-pkm-fd-pfd-x-t13` (claude/serene-davinci-ujoi58).
  - 14 raw `records.jsonl` files in `20261110T2210Z-sib-cv0-stage1-eks`, `20261111T0020Z-sib-stage2-primefield`, `20261111T0151Z-sib-stage3-scaling` (branch claude/best-leads-experiments-jikl0j): not in Git, `uploaded: false`, absent from their store URI. Their `.artifacts.json` manifests list only `records.jsonl.gz`, which is in the store, so the original bytes are probably recoverable (gunzip, check sha256 and size, upload to the recorded key, add a manifest entry). Not done here: it edits other studies' directories and writes to the shared bucket.
- Is `records` a required check? The API for that was not reachable from this session.


- The first background timing run of the old code was blocked by the checkpoint hook, so the old copy was never written. Re-ran with `CHECKPOINT_SKIP` (CI diagnosis, not an experiment).

## E-222 / M-221 regimen: native-size band as a model projection (Claude)

- User said "go go go" to the native-size band. A real solve at E-222 / M-221 needs more than 2^100 operations, so the band at native size can only be a projection; I wrote the counter model first and checked it against the 120 measured band runs before projecting.
- Measured the two scalings the model needs on cofactor-1 instances of 16 to 17 bits (F = 8 to 256, table forced): trials per relation fall as F^-2 until the table saturates (530, 136, 29, 8, 2.5, 1.2), elimination operations grow as about 4.7 F^2 (422, 1504, 4846, 16742, 71006, 309608).
- `native_band/native_band_model.py` (stdlib): validation against the panel: table inserts and negations exact; trials / probes / scalars median observed / predicted 0.931 (default) and 1.032 (H1), IQR about +-10%; group operations 0.93 to 1.03; square roots 0.91 to 0.93; elimination 0.87 to 1.00.
- Projection to the real E-222 (220 bits, cofactor 4) and M-221 (218 bits, cofactor 8), F = 2^5 to 2^30, with and without H1, w = 0 / 0.1 / 1: IC about 2^187 to 2^225 GAE (trial term dominates; F^2 table, elimination and weight w are negligible: the IC cost differs by at most 0.011 in log2 between w = 0 and 1); matched rho about 2^110 to 2^111. At F = 2^20 with H1: 2^207.6 (E-222), 2^205.6 (M-221). `RESULTS_native_band.md`, `native_band/native_band_results.json`.
- Added claim C6 to the evidence bundle and to `recompute.py`; recompute passes for all six claims; gate tool still refuses the draft only for the two `independent: false` flags and the robustness rows in the artifact store.

- Labelled it a model projection in every place it appears; reported the distance, no overall verdict.
- Fixed in the note before committing: a stray escape sequence in the generated text, and a sentence that claimed w changes nothing when it moves the distance by about half a bit (through rho's own unpriced operations).

- The model is checked only up to n of 21 bits; a mid-size measured point (a 30 to 40 bit subgroup, which `solve-prime-file` cannot take because of its field cap of 4e7) would test it further; independent replay / validator with keyring signatures; robustness rows into the repository; pcc claims; EKS.

- None that reached a commit.

## 06:30Z — How often curves land in the JV weak class; the implemented walk reaches few of them

- `research/jv_weak_coverage_20261111/`: the protocol was frozen at 1b824d8168, followed by two amendments during the runs, both disclosed.
- New example `examples/jv_weak_coverage.rs`. For each curve it samples a random full-2-torsion curve, counts its points by baby-step giant-step, applies the proved trace condition and, for admitted classes, runs `walk_to_weak_record` with jumps 3, 5, 7.
- U, PARI/GP `ellcard` (`uniform.gp`): 2,000 uniform curves per prime at p ∈ {37, 53, 1009, 1511, 1823}. 22.3–24.5 % are admitted (`t ≡ ±(p⁶+1) mod 16`), flat in p.
- F, 400 curves with full 2-torsion per prime: 59–63 % admitted.
- W, 60 admitted classes per prime, cap 10,000 curves. The walk reaches a weak curve in 40 % / 42 % of classes at p = 37 / 53, 3 of 60 at p = 1009, and 0 of 60 at p = 1511 and 1823.
- Successful random-start walks at p = 1009 took 19–153 steps and cost 1.8e9–3.0e10 multiplications, i.e. 0.008–0.14 × rho.
- Figure `coverage.svg`/`png` and `REPORT.pdf` written.
- Added the reachability caveat to `research/jv_walk_charge_20261111/README.md` and to the leaderboard `walk_note`. The leaderboard was regenerated and `--check` passes.

- The coverage upper bound is the admitted share of uniform curves, about 23 %. The census truth (98.8–99.6 % of admitted classes hold a weak curve at p ≤ 67) is quoted, not re-measured at p ≥ 1009.
- Operational coverage of the route as implemented is about 1 % of curves at p = 1009. The walk charge holds only for starts within the walk's reach.

- A class-group walk: odd isogenies from kernel polynomials of degree (ℓ−1)/2, not only rational kernel points. It is needed to reach weak curves from arbitrary admitted curves, and to measure the true reach cost.
- The quadratic-h branch of the published family is not covered.

- Amendment 1: walking every admitted curve with a 100,000-curve cap was too slow (single walks up to 195 s).
- Amendment 2: at p = 1009 one walk ran for more than 10 minutes under the 100,000 cap, so the cap was lowered to 10,000.
